Problem

View Problem Statement 
You are an expert Python programmer. You will be given a question (problem specification) and will generate a correct Python program that matches the specification and passes all tests.

Recently Vladik discovered a new entertainment — coding bots for social networks. He would like to use machine learning in his bots so now he want to prepare some learning data for them.

At first, he need to download t chats. Vladik coded a script which should have downloaded the chats, however, something went wrong. In particular, some of the messages have no information of their sender. It is known that if a person sends several messages in a row, they all are merged into a single message. It means that there could not be two or more messages in a row with the same sender. Moreover, a sender never mention himself in his messages.

Vladik wants to recover senders of all the messages so that each two neighboring messages will have different senders and no sender will mention himself in his messages.

He has no idea of how to do this, and asks you for help. Help Vladik to recover senders in each of the chats!


-----Input-----

The first line contains single integer t (1 ≤ t ≤ 10) — the number of chats. The t chats follow. Each chat is given in the following format.

The first line of each chat description contains single integer n (1 ≤ n ≤ 100) — the number of users in the chat.

The next line contains n space-separated distinct usernames. Each username consists of lowercase and uppercase English letters and digits. The usernames can't start with a digit. Two usernames are different even if they differ only with letters' case. The length of username is positive and doesn't exceed 10 characters.

The next line contains single integer m (1 ≤ m ≤ 100) — the number of messages in the chat. The next m line contain the messages in the following formats, one per line:   <username>:<text> — the format of a message with known sender. The username should appear in the list of usernames of the chat.  <?>:<text> — the format of a message with unknown sender. 

The text of a message can consist of lowercase and uppercase English letter, digits, characters '.' (dot), ',' (comma), '!' (exclamation mark), '?' (question mark) and ' ' (space). The text doesn't contain trailing spaces. The length of the text is positive and doesn't exceed 100 characters.

We say that a text mention a user if his username appears in the text as a word. In other words, the username appears in a such a position that the two characters before and after its appearance either do not exist or are not English letters or digits. For example, the text "Vasya, masha13 and Kate!" can mention users "Vasya", "masha13", "and" and "Kate", but not "masha".

It is guaranteed that in each chat no known sender mention himself in his messages and there are no two neighboring messages with the same known sender.


-----Output-----

Print the information about the t chats in the following format:

If it is not possible to recover senders, print single line "Impossible" for this chat. Otherwise print m messages in the following format:

<username>:<text>

If there are multiple answers, print any of them.


-----Examples-----
Input
1
2
Vladik netman
2
?: Hello, Vladik!
?: Hi

Output
netman: Hello, Vladik!
Vladik: Hi

Input
1
2
netman vladik
3
netman:how are you?
?:wrong message
vladik:im fine

Output
Impossible

?: users are netman, vladik, Fedosik
vladik: something wrong with this chat

Model Response

View Full Response 
I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have chats with messages that may have unknown senders (marked as `?`)
2. Each message must have a sender different from its neighbors
3. A sender cannot mention themselves in their message (mention means the username appears as a word)
4. We need to assign senders to all messages with `?` such that all constraints are satisfied

## Key Insights

1. The constraint that "if a person sends several messages in a row, they all are merged into a single message" means no two adjacent messages can have the same sender.
2. A sender cannot mention themselves in their message (the username must not appear as a word in the text).
3. We need to solve this constraint satisfaction problem.

## Algorithm Approach

1. For each chat, we'll process messages one by one
2. For messages with known senders, we already know who sent them
3. For messages with `?`, we need to assign a sender that:
   - Is different from its neighbors (previous and next messages)
   - Does not mention the assigned username in the text
4. We can use a greedy approach: for each unknown message, try each possible sender and check if it violates any constraints
5. If no valid assignment is found, output "Impossible"

## Step-by-step Solution

```python
def extract_mentions(text, usernames):
    """Extract all words that match usernames from the text"""
    mentions = []
    words = []
    
    # Split text into words (using only alphanumeric characters and spaces)
    i = 0
    while i < len(text):
        if text[i].isalnum():
            start = i
            while i < len(text) and text[i].isalnum():
                i += 1
            words.append(text[start:i])
        else:
            i += 1
    
    # Check which words are in the usernames list
    for word in words:
        if word in usernames:
            mentions.append(word)
    
    return mentions

def can_sender_mention(sender, text, usernames):
    """Check if a sender would mention themselves in the text"""
    mentions = extract_mentions(text, usernames)
    return sender not in mentions

def is_valid_assignment(messages, usernames, known_senders, unknown_positions):
    """Check if we can assign senders to unknown messages given constraints"""
    n = len(messages)
    result = []
    
    # First, create a copy of messages with senders
    current_senders = []
    
    for i in range(n):
        if i < len(known_senders):
            current_senders.append(known_senders[i])
        else:
            current_senders.append(None)
    
    # Try to assign senders to unknown messages
    for pos in unknown_positions:
        # Try each possible sender
        valid = False
        for sender in usernames:
            # Check constraints
            # 1. Not same as previous message
            if pos > 0 and current_senders[pos-1] == sender:
                continue
            
            # 2. Not same as next message (if exists)
            if pos < n-1 and current_senders[pos+1] == sender:
                continue
                
            # 3. Sender doesn't mention themselves
            if not can_sender_mention(sender, messages[pos][1], usernames):
                continue
                
            # If we reach here, this sender works
            current_senders[pos] = sender
            valid = True
            break
        
        if not valid:
            return False
    
    # Verify all constraints are satisfied
    for i in range(n):
        if current_senders[i] is not None:
            # Check no two adjacent same senders
            if i > 0 and current_senders[i] == current_senders[i-1]:
                return False
            if i < n-1 and current_senders[i] == current_senders[i+1]:
                return False
            
            # Check sender doesn't mention themselves
            if not can_sender_mention(current_senders[i], messages[i][1], usernames):
                return False
    
    return True

def solve_chat(n, usernames, messages):
    # Parse messages
    message_list = []
    known_senders = []
    unknown_positions = []
    
    for i, msg in enumerate(messages):
        if msg.startswith('?'):
            message_list.append((None, msg[2:]))
            unknown_positions.append(i)
        else:
            parts = msg.split(':', 1)
            sender = parts[0]
            text = parts[1]
            message_list.append((sender, text))
            known_senders.append(sender)
    
    # Check if the known senders already violate constraints
    for i in range(len(known_senders)):
        if i > 0 and known_senders[i] == known_senders[i-1]:
            return "Impossible"
        if i < len(known_senders) - 1 and known_senders[i] == known_senders[i+1]:
            return "Impossible"
    
    # For each known sender, check if they mention themselves
    for i, sender in enumerate(known_senders):
        if not can_sender_mention(sender, message_list[i][1], usernames):
            return "Impossible"
    
    # Try to assign senders to unknown messages
    # We'll use a greedy approach with backtracking for small cases
    result = message_list[:]
    
    # Try to assign senders greedily
    for pos in unknown_positions:
        assigned = False
        for sender in usernames:
            # Check adjacent constraints
            prev_same = (pos > 0 and result[pos-1][0] == sender)
            next_same = (pos < len(result) - 1 and result[pos+1][0] == sender)
            
            # Check self-mention
            if not can_sender_mention(sender, result[pos][1], usernames):
                continue
                
            if not prev_same and not next_same:
                result[pos] = (sender, result[pos][1])
                assigned = True
                break
        
        if not assigned:
            return "Impossible"
    
    # Final validation
    for i in range(len(result)):
        if i > 0 and result[i][0] == result[i-1][0]:
            return "Impossible"
        if i < len(result) - 1 and result[i][0] == result[i+1][0]:
            return "Impossible"
        if result[i][0] is not None and not can_sender_mention(result[i][0], result[i][1], usernames):
            return "Impossible"
    
    return result

def main():
    t = int(input())
    
    for _ in range(t):
        n = int(input())
        usernames = input().split()
        m = int(input())
        
        messages = []
        for _ in range(m):
            messages.append(input())
        
        result = solve_chat(n, usernames, messages)
        
        if result == "Impossible":
            print("Impossible")
        else:
            for msg in result:
                print(f"{msg[0]}:{msg[1]}")

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

```python
def extract_mentions(text, usernames):
    """Extract all words that match usernames from the text"""
    words = []
    i = 0
    while i < len(text):
        if text[i].isalnum():
            start = i
            while i < len(text) and text[i].isalnum():
                i += 1
            words.append(text[start:i])
        else:
            i += 1
    
    return [word for word in words if word in usernames]

def can_sender_mention(sender, text, usernames):
    """Check if a sender would mention themselves in the text"""
    mentions = extract_mentions(text, usernames)
    return sender not in mentions

def solve_chat():
    n = int(input())
    usernames = input().split()
    m = int(input())
    
    messages = []
    for _ in range(m):
        messages.append(input())
    
    # Create a list to store the final sender for each message
    senders = []
    
    # Process each message
    for i, msg in enumerate(messages):
        if msg.startswith('?'):
            senders.append(None)
        else:
            sender_part = msg.split(':', 1)[0]
            senders.append(sender_part)
    
    # Check if known senders already violate constraints
    for i in range(len(senders)):
        if i > 0 and senders[i] == senders[i-1]:
            return "Impossible"
        if i < len(senders) - 1 and senders[i] == senders[i+1]:
            return "Impossible"
    
    # Check if any known sender mentions themselves
    for i, sender in enumerate(senders):
        if sender is not None and not can_sender_mention(sender, messages[i], usernames):
            return "Impossible"
    
    # Now assign senders to messages with ?
    # We'll use a greedy approach: try each possible sender for each unknown message
    for i in range(len(senders)):
        if senders[i] is None:
            # Try each username as a potential sender
            assigned = False
            for username in usernames:
                # Check if this username works
                # 1. Not same as previous message
                prev_same = (i > 0 and senders[i-1] == username)
                # 2. Not same as next message (if exists)
                next_same = (i < len(senders) - 1 and senders[i+1] == username)
                # 3. Sender doesn't mention themselves
                
                if not prev_same and not next_same and can_sender_mention(username, messages[i], usernames):
                    senders[i] = username
                    assigned = True
                    break
            
            if not assigned:
                return "Impossible"
    
    # Final check: verify all constraints
    for i in range(len(senders)):
        if i > 0 and senders[i] == senders[i-1]:
            return "Impossible"
        if i < len(senders) - 1 and senders[i] == senders[i+1]:
            return "Impossible"
        if senders[i] is not None and not can_sender_mention(senders[i], messages[i], usernames):
            return "Impossible"
    
    # Format output
    result = []
    for i, msg in enumerate(messages):
        if senders[i] is None:
            result.append(f"{senders[i]}:{msg[2:]}")
        else:
            result.append(f"{senders[i]}:{msg}")
    
    return result

# Main execution
t = int(input())
for _ in range(t):
    result = solve_chat()
    if result == "Impossible":
        print("Impossible")
    else:
        for msg in result:
            print(msg)
```

def extract_mentions(text, usernames):
    """Extract all words that match usernames from the text"""
    words = []
    i = 0
    while i < len(text):
        if text[i].isalnum():
            start = i
            while i < len(text) and text[i].isalnum():
                i += 1
            words.append(text[start:i])
        else:
            i += 1
    
    return [word for word in words if word in usernames]

def can_sender_mention(sender, text, usernames):
    """Check if a sender would mention themselves in the text"""
    mentions = extract_mentions(text, usernames)
    return sender not in mentions

def solve_chat():
    n = int(input())
    usernames = input().split()
    m = int(input())
    
    messages = []
    for _ in range(m):
        messages.append(input())
    
    # Create a list to store the final sender for each message
    senders = []
    
    # Process each message
    for i, msg in enumerate(messages):
        if msg.startswith('?'):
            senders.append(None)
        else:
            sender_part = msg.split(':', 1)[0]
            senders.append(sender_part)
    
    # Check if known senders already violate constraints
    for i in range(len(senders)):
        if i > 0 and senders[i] == senders[i-1]:
            return "Impossible"
        if i < len(senders) - 1 and senders[i] == senders[i+1]:
            return "Impossible"
    
    # Check if any known sender mentions themselves
    for i, sender in enumerate(senders):
        if sender is not None and not can_sender_mention(sender, messages[i], usernames):
            return "Impossible"
    
    # Now assign senders to messages with ?
    # We'll use a greedy approach: try each possible sender for each unknown message
    for i in range(len(senders)):
        if senders[i] is None:
            # Try each username as a potential sender
            assigned = False
            for username in usernames:
                # Check if this username works
                # 1. Not same as previous message
                prev_same = (i > 0 and senders[i-1] == username)
                # 2. Not same as next message (if exists)
                next_same = (i < len(senders) - 1 and senders[i+1] == username)
                # 3. Sender doesn't mention themselves
                
                if not prev_same and not next_same and can_sender_mention(username, messages[i], usernames):
                    senders[i] = username
                    assigned = True
                    break
            
            if not assigned:
                return "Impossible"
    
    # Final check: verify all constraints
    for i in range(len(senders)):
        if i > 0 and senders[i] == senders[i-1]:
            return "Impossible"
        if i < len(senders) - 1 and senders[i] == senders[i+1]:
            return "Impossible"
        if senders[i] is not None and not can_sender_mention(senders[i], messages[i], usernames):
            return "Impossible"
    
    # Format output
    result = []
    for i, msg in enumerate(messages):
        if senders[i] is None:
            result.append(f"{senders[i]}:{msg[2:]}")
        else:
            result.append(f"{senders[i]}:{msg}")
    
    return result

# Main execution

        print("Impossible")
    else:

            print(msg)
